, and

•

(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{41}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{42}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{51}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{52}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

(7)

By (
R_Succeed
) and (
E_Step
),
there exist
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{61}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{62}}}}
such that

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{61}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!X_{{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!X_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
,

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{62}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!X_{{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!X_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
, and

•

(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{51}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{52}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{61}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{62}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

(8)

By (
R_Ground
) and (
E_Step
),
there exist
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
such that

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!X_{{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!X_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
,

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!X_{{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!X_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
, and

•

(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{61}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{62}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,(\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

Finally,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[X:=X_{{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!X_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,(\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.
∎

Definition A.3.

\Omega^{X}
is a set of terms:

\Omega^{X}=\{(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{m}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)\mid\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}\,\wedge\,\min\limits_{m,n}(\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{m}}),\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{n}}))>0\,\wedge\,m,n\geq 0\}
.

Lemma A.7.

For any
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]
where
\mathit{f}\in\Omega^{X}
,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime}}
,
\mathit{f^{\prime}}
, and
S
such that

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]
where
\mathit{f^{\prime}}\in\Omega^{X}
.

Proof.

We are given
\mathit{f}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
where
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}
.
By case analysis on
\mathit{i}
.

Case
i=0
:
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.

By (
R_Beta
) and (
E_Step
),

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime}}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.

By (
R_Succeed
),

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.

Finally,
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}]
.
Obviously,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\in\Omega^{X}
.

Case
i>0
:
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.

Here
\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}})>0
, so there exist
U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}}
and
U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}

such that
U^{X}_{\mathit{i}}=U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}
.

By (
R_AppCast
) and (
E_Step
),

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}:U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime}}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}})\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}})
.

Case
\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}})=1
:
:

Here,
\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}})=0
. There exists
\mathit{X}
such that
U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}}=\mathit{X}
.
By evaluating
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]
using the rules (
R_InstArrow
), (
R_Succeed
), (
R_AppCast
), and (
E_Step
),

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}:U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}]

where

S=[\mathit{X}:=\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]
and

\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!S(U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!S(U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!S(U^{X}_{\mathit{j}})\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!S(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}})\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.
Obviously,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\in\Omega^{X}
.

Case
\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}})>1
:
:

Here,
\textit{ord}\,(U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}})>0
.
By evaluating
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]
using (
R_Expand
), (
R_AppCast
), and (
E_Step
),

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}:U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{2}}}]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}=(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{j}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{X}_{\mathit{i}\,{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.
Obviously,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\in\Omega^{X}
.
∎

Lemma A.8.

If

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!X.\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)):X\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, and

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
,

then
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\Uparrow
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
A.6
and
A.7
.
∎

Lemma A.9.

If

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!U^{\iota}.\,(\mathit{x}:U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{x}:U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)):U^{\iota}\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star

\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota})>0

then

By applying reduction rules and evaluation rules.

Definition A.4.

\Omega^{\iota}_{i}
is a set of terms:

\Omega^{\iota}_{i}=\{\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)\mid\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\,\wedge\,\min\limits_{n}(\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}))=\iota\,\wedge\,n\geq 0\}
.

Lemma A.10.

For any
\mathit{E}
,
w_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
w_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
where
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}})>1
,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime}}
and
U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}
such that

\mathit{E}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime}}[w_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]

and
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}})=\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}})-1
.

Proof.

There exist
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
and
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
such that
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{12}}}}

because
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}})>1
.
By definition,
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}})=\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}})-1
.

\mathit{E}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]
is evaluated as follows:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&\mathit{E}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]\\
\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&\mathit{E^{\prime}}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}})\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}})]\\
&\textrm{where $\mathit{E^{\prime}}=\mathit{E}[\left[\,\right]:U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\,\star]$}\\
\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&\mathit{E^{\prime}}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}})\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}})]\\
\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&\mathit{E^{\prime}}[(w_{{\mathrm{1}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}})\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}})]\\
\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&\mathit{E^{\prime\prime}}[w_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]\\
&\textrm{where $\mathit{E^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\left[\,\right]:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\,U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{12}}}]$}\\
\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&\mathit{E^{\prime\prime}}[w_{{\mathrm{1}}}\,(w_{{\mathrm{2}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)]\end{array}

∎

Lemma A.11.

For any
\mathit{E}
and
\mathit{f}\in\Omega^{\iota}_{i}
where
i>1
,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime}}
such that

\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]
and
\mathit{f^{\prime}}\in\Omega^{\iota}_{i-1}
.

Proof.

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
and

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.

By applying Lemma
A.10

n
times,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime}}
such that
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime}}=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,
m=2n
,

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}
, and

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{m}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.

Then,
\min\limits_{1\leq i\leq m}\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{\mathit{i}})=\min\limits_{1\leq i\leq n}\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{\mathit{i}})-1
.

By applying reduction and evaluation rules,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime\prime}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}]

where
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,

•

\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{m}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, and

•

\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{m}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.

Finally,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\in\Omega^{\iota}_{i-1}
.
∎

\mathit{E}

,

Lemma A.13.

For any
\mathit{E}
and
\mathit{f}\in\Omega^{\iota}_{1}
,
there exists
\ell
such that
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
.

Proof.

There exist
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
such that
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, and

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{n}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.

And, there exists
i
such that
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota}_{\mathit{i}})=1
.

By applying Lemma
A.10

n-i
times,
there exist
\mathit{E^{\prime}}
,
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
such that
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]
,

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{i}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, and

•

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\cdots\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}_{\mathit{m}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.

By Lemma
A.12
,

\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
.
Finally,
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
.
∎

Lemma A.14.

For any
S
such that
\textit{ftv}\,(S(X))=\emptyset
,

S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
for some
\ell
where

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!X.\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)):X\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
and

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

Proof.

There exists
\mathit{T}
such that
S(X)=\mathit{T}
.
Here,
\textit{ftv}\,(S(X))=\emptyset
.
So, there exists
U^{\iota}
such that
S(X)=U^{\iota}
,

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!U^{\iota}.\,(\mathit{x}:U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{x}:U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)):U^{\iota}\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, and

•

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

By case analysis on the order of
U^{\iota}
.

Case
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota})=0
:
:

There exists
\iota
such that
U^{\iota}=\iota
.
Obviously,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
for some
\ell
.

Case
\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota})>0
:
:

By Lemma
A.9
,
There exist

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
and

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!U^{\iota}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
,

such that
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,(\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.

Here
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\in\Omega^{\iota}_{n}
where
n=\textit{ord}\,(U^{\iota})
.
By Lemma
A.11
and
Lemma
A.13
. We finish.
∎

\thmDivergence

*

Proof.

Let
\mathit{f}=(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!X.\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,(\mathit{x}:X\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)):X\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\,(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\star.\,(\mathit{x}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star)\,\mathit{x}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.
By Lemma
A.8
,
\mathit{f}\!\Uparrow
.

Here,
\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f})=\{X\}
.
By Lemma
A.14
,
for any
S
where
\textit{ftv}\,(S(X))=\emptyset
,

S(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
for some
\ell
.

By Theorem
6
,
S(\mathit{f})\,\longmapsto_{\textsf{{B}}}^{\ast}\,\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
.
∎

A.4.
Completeness of Dynamic Type Inference

Lemma A.15.

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:\mathit{U}
and
S(\mathit{f})
is a value,
then
\mathit{f}
is a value.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation.

Case (
T_VarP
):
:

Cannot happen.

Case (
T_Const
), (
T_Abs
):
:

Obvious.

Case (
T_Op
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{op}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
for some
\mathit{op}
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

S(\mathit{f})=\mathit{op}\,(S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
T_App
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
for some
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\,S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
T_Cast
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U}
for some
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,
\mathit{U^{\prime}}
and
\ell
.
By inversion,
\emptyset\vdash_{\textsf{{B}}}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{U^{\prime}}\sim\mathit{U}
.
By case analysis on the type consistency relation.

Case (
C_Base
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\iota
for some
\iota
.

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\iota
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
C_TyVar
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{X}
for some
\mathit{X}
.

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\mathit{T}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{T}
for some
\mathit{T}
.

By case analysis on the structure of
\mathit{T}
.

Case
\mathit{T}=\iota
for some
\iota
:
:

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\iota
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case
\mathit{T}=\mathit{X^{\prime}}
for some
\mathit{X^{\prime}}
:
:

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\mathit{X^{\prime}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{X^{\prime}}
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case
\mathit{T}=\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
for some
\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
:
:

We are given
S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{T_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
By Lemma
2
,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
is a value.
By the IH,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
is a value.

By Lemma
2
, the value
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
is not typed at a type variable.
Contradiction.

Case (
C_DynL
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U}
.

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!S(\mathit{U})
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
C_DynR
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.
By Lemma
2
,

S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
is a value and
S(\mathit{U^{\prime}})
is a ground type.
By the IH,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
is a value.

By case analysis on the structure of
\mathit{U^{\prime}}
.

Case
\mathit{U^{\prime}}=\star
:
:

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case
\mathit{U^{\prime}}=\iota
:
:

We are given
S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
.
So,
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
is a value.

Case
\mathit{U^{\prime}}=\mathit{X}
:
:

By Lemma
2
, the value
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
is not typed at a type variable.
Contradiction.

Case
\mathit{U^{\prime}}=\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
:
:

By definition,
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
is a value.

Case
\mathit{U}=\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\mathit{U}\neq\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
:
:

S(\mathit{f})=S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):S(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
C_Arrow
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

for some
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

where
\mathit{U}=\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\mathit{U^{\prime}}=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

By Lemma
2
,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
is a value.
By the IH,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
is a value.

Finally,
\mathit{f}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
is a value.

Case (
T_LetP
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{1}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

for some
\mathit{x}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}
,
w_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
Obviously,
S(\mathit{f})
is not a value.
Contradiction.

Case (
T_Blame
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f}=\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
for some
\ell
.
For any
S
,
S(\mathit{f})=\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
. Contradiction.
∎

\lemCompletenessValueEvalStep

*

Proof.

By case analysis on the evaluation rule applied to
S(\mathit{f})
.

Case (
E_Step
):
:

There exist
\mathit{E}
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
such that

S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,

S(\mathit{E})[S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,[](S(\mathit{E})[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])
,

\mathit{f}=\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime}}=S(\mathit{E})[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]
.

By Lemma
A.17
,
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
for some
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
By case analysis on the typing derivation on
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Case (
T_VarP
), (
T_Const
), (
T_Abs
), (
T_Blame
):
:

Cannot happen.

Case (
T_Op
):
:

We are given
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{op}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}},\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}):\iota_{{\mathrm{1}}}

for some
\mathit{op}
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
, and
\iota_{{\mathrm{1}}}

where
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\iota_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
By inversion, we have
\mathit{ty}(\mathit{op})=\iota_{{\mathrm{11}}}\!\rightarrow\!\iota_{{\mathrm{12}}}\!\rightarrow\!\iota_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,

\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\iota_{{\mathrm{11}}}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\iota_{{\mathrm{12}}}

for some
\iota_{{\mathrm{11}}}
and
\iota_{{\mathrm{12}}}
.
By case analysis on the reduction rule applied to
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
.

Case (
R_Op
):
:

We are given
\mathit{op}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}},w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\llbracket\mathit{op}\rrbracket(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}},w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}})

where
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}
,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}})=w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}
, and
S^{\prime}=[]
.
By Lemma
A.15
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
are values.
So, there exist
w_{{\mathrm{11}}}
and
w_{{\mathrm{12}}}

such that
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}=w_{{\mathrm{11}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}=w_{{\mathrm{12}}}
.
By (
R_Op
),
\mathit{op}\,(w_{{\mathrm{11}}},w_{{\mathrm{12}}})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\llbracket\mathit{op}\rrbracket(w_{{\mathrm{11}}},w_{{\mathrm{12}}})
.
By (
E_Step
),
\mathit{E}[\mathit{op}\,(w_{{\mathrm{11}}},w_{{\mathrm{12}}})]\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,[](\mathit{E}[\llbracket\mathit{op}\rrbracket(w_{{\mathrm{11}}},w_{{\mathrm{12}}})])
.

Let
S^{\prime\prime}=S
, then
S(\mathit{E}[\mathit{op}\,(w_{{\mathrm{11}}},w_{{\mathrm{12}}})])\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S(\mathit{E})[\llbracket\mathit{op}\rrbracket(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}},w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}})]
.

Otherwise:
:

Cannot happen.

Case (
T_App
):
:

We are given
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
for some
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
.
By inversion, we have
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

for some
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
By case analysis on the reduction rule applied to
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
.

Case (
R_Beta
):
:

We are given
(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})\,w^{\prime}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=w^{\prime}]

where
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}})=w^{\prime}
,

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=w^{\prime}]
, and
S^{\prime}=[]
.
By definition,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}=\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}

where
S(\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
S(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
.
By Lemma
A.15
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
is a value.
By (
R_Beta
),
(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]
.
Also,
S(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}])=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=w^{\prime}]
.
By (
E_Step
),
\mathit{E}[(\lambda\mathit{x}\!:\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,[](\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]])
.
Let
S^{\prime\prime}=S
, then
S^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]])=S(\mathit{E})[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}[\mathit{x}:=w^{\prime}]]
.

Case (
R_AppCast
):
:

We are given
(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}})\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}

where
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}
,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}})=w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}
,

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}
,
and
S^{\prime}=[]
.
By definition,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}=\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}

where
S(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}
,
S(\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
,
S(\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}})=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
,

S(\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}})=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}
, and
S(\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}})=\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}
.
By Lemma
A.15
,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
are values.
By (
R_AppCast
),

(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}})\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}
.
Also,
S((\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}})=(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}
.
By (
E_Step
),

\mathit{E}[(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}})\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,[](\mathit{E}[(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}])
.
Let
S^{\prime\prime}=S
, then

S^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{E}[(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}])=S(\mathit{E})[(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\,(w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{13}}}}\Rightarrow^{\bar{\ell}}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}})):\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{14}}}}]
.

Otherwise:
:

Cannot happen.

Case (
T_Cast
):
:

We are given
\emptyset\vdash(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}):\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

for some
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
,
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\ell
.
By inversion, we have
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sim\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
By case analysis on
\mathit{U^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\sim\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Case (
C_Base
):
:

We are given
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{11}}}}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\iota
for some
\iota
.
By definition,

T_LetP
):
:

We are given
\emptyset\vdash\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U}

for some
\mathit{x}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}
,
w_{{\mathrm{11}}}
, and
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
.
By inversion, we have
\emptyset\vdash w_{{\mathrm{11}}}:\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{11}}}}
,

\emptyset,\mathit{x}:\forall\,\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{11}}}}\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}:\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}\cap\textit{ftv}\,(\Gamma)=\emptyset
.

By case analysis on the reduction rule applied to
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
.

Case (
R_LetP
):
:

We are given
\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}]
where

S(\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}})=\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}
and

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}]
.
By (
R_LetP
),

\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}]
.
Also,
S(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}])=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}]
.
By (
E_Step
),
\mathit{E}[\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,[](\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}]])
.
Let
S^{\prime\prime}=S
, then
S^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w_{{\mathrm{11}}}]])=S(\mathit{E})[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{12}}}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X_{\mathit{i}}}}.\,w^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{11}}}]]
.

Case (
E_Abort
):
:

Here,
\mathit{f^{\prime}}=\textsf{{blame}}\,\ell
for some
\ell
. Contradiction.
∎

Lemma A.16.

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:\mathit{U}
and
S(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w
,
then
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime\prime\prime}(w^{\prime})=w

for some
w^{\prime}
,
S^{\prime\prime}
, and
S^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

By mathematical induction on the length of the evaluation sequence.

Case the length is 0:
:

Here,
S(\mathit{f})=w
.
By Lemma
A.15
,
\mathit{f}
is a value
w
.
Then,
w\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w
.

Case the length is more than 0:
:

There exist
\mathit{f^{\prime}}
,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that
S(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w

and
S^{\prime}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

By Lemma
4
,
there exist
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}
,
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

such that
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}})=\mathit{f^{\prime}}
.

Here,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w

and its evaluation sequence is shorter than
that of
S(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w
.
Also, by Lemma
4
,
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{U}
.
By the IH,
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,w^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(w^{\prime})=w
for some
S_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Finally,
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(w^{\prime})=w
.
∎

\thmCompleteness

*

Proof.

(1)

By Lemma
A.16
.

(2)

Let
n
be an arbitrary natural number and suppose
S(\mathit{f})\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\cdots\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\mathit{f^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}
.
By repeatedly applying Lemma
4
,
we obtain
\mathit{f}\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\cdots\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{\mathit{n}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\mathit{f_{\mathit{n}}}
and
type substitutions
S^{\prime\prime}_{\mathit{i}}
such that
S^{\prime\prime}_{\mathit{i}}(\mathit{f_{\mathit{i}}})=\mathit{f^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}
.
∎

A.5.
Soundness of Dynamic Type Inference

Lemma A.17.

If
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]:\mathit{U}
,
then
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
for some
\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathit{E}
.
∎

Lemma A.18.

If
\emptyset\vdash w:\mathit{U}
and
w
is a value,
then
S(w)
is a value for any
S
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the structure of
w
.
∎

Lemma A.19.

If

\mathit{f}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and

\textit{dom}\,(S)\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
and

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
are disjoint from type variables generated by

\mathit{f}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
,
then

S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(\mathit{f^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the reduction rule applied to derive
\mathit{f}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
.

Case (
R_OP
), (
R_Beta
), (
R_IdBase
), (
R_IdStar
), (
R_Succeed
), (
R_Fail
), and (
R_AppCast
):
:

Easy.

Case (
R_Ground
):
:

We are given
w:\mathit{U}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w:\mathit{U}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{G}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star

for some
w
,
\mathit{U}
,
\ell
, and
\mathit{G}
such that

\mathit{U}\neq\star
and
\mathit{U}\neq\mathit{G}
and
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.
By case analysis on
\mathit{U}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\iota
:
:

Contradictory since
\mathit{U}\neq\mathit{G}
and
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\star
:
:

Contradictory.

Case
\mathit{U}=\mathit{X}
:
:

Contradictory since
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
:
:

We have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{U})=S^{\prime}(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
and
\mathit{G}=\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.
Since
\mathit{U}\neq\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, we have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{U})\neq\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.
Since
S^{\prime}(w)
is a value by Lemma
A.18
,
we have
S^{\prime}(w:\mathit{U}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(w:\mathit{U}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{G}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star)
.

Case (
R_Expand
):
:

We are given
w:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{G}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U}

for some
w
,
\mathit{U}
,
\ell
, and
\mathit{G}
such that

\mathit{U}\neq\star
and
\mathit{U}\neq\mathit{G}
and
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.
By case analysis on
\mathit{U}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\iota
:
:

Contradictory since
\mathit{U}\neq\mathit{G}
and
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\star
:
:

Contradictory.

Case
\mathit{U}=\mathit{X}
:
:

Contradictory since
\mathit{U}\sim\mathit{G}
.

Case
\mathit{U}=\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
:
:

We have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{U})=S^{\prime}(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}(\mathit{U_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
and
\mathit{G}=\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.
Since
\mathit{U}\neq\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
, we have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{U})\neq\star\!\rightarrow\!\star
.
Since
S^{\prime}(w)
is a value by Lemma
A.18
,
we have
S^{\prime}(w:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(w:\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{G}\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\mathit{U})
.

Case (
R_InstBase
):
:

We are given
w:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[\mathit{X}:=\iota]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w

for some
w
,
\iota
,
\mathit{X}
,
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
\{\mathit{X}\}\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
,
we have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})=S^{\prime}(w):\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[\mathit{X}:=\iota]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(w)
.

Case (
R_InstArrow
):
:

We are given
w:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[\mathit{X}:=\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,w:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

for some
w
,
\mathit{X}
,
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and fresh
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
Without loss of generality, we can suppose that
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
.
Since
\{\mathit{X}\}\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
,
we have
S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})=S^{\prime}(w):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[\mathit{X}:=\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(w):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
Since
S^{\prime}(w):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=S^{\prime}(w:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
, we finish.

Case (
R_LetP
):
:

We are given
\textsf{{let}}\,\mathit{x}=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X}}.\,w^{\prime\prime}\textsf{{ in }}\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}[\mathit{x}:=\Lambda\overrightarrow{\mathit{X}}.\,w^{\prime\prime}]
.
Type variables generated by the value substitution are supposed not to be
captured by
S^{\prime}
.
Thus, we finish. ∎

Lemma A.20 (name=,restate=lemEvalStepAnySubst).

If
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and

\textit{dom}\,(S)\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
and

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
are disjoint from type variables generated by

\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and,
for any
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
,
\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}(\mathit{X}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S)=\emptyset
,
then

S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(\mathit{f^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By case analysis on the evaluation rule applied to
\mathit{f}
.

Case (
E_Step
):
:

We are given
\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])

for some
\mathit{E}
,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
such that

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
By Lemma
A.19
,

S^{\prime}(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
.
By (
E_Step
),

S^{\prime}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S(S^{\prime}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))
.
By the assumption,
S(S^{\prime}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))=S^{\prime}(S(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))
.
Thus, we finish.

By Lemma
A.19

Case (
E_Abort
):
:

Obvious. ∎

Lemma A.21.

If

\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and

\textit{dom}\,(S)\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
and

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
are disjoint from type variables generated by

\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
and,
for any
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
,
\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}(\mathit{X}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S)=\emptyset
,
then

S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,S^{\prime}(\mathit{f^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

By induction on the length of the evaluation sequence.

Case the length is 0:
:

Obvious.

Case the length is more than 0:
:

We are given
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}
and
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}

for some
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
S_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}}
such that

S=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
Thus,
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})=\emptyset
and,
for any
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime})
,
\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}(\mathit{X}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\emptyset
.
Hence, by Lemma
A.20
,

S^{\prime}(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S^{\prime}(\mathit{f^{\prime\prime}})
.
By the IH, we finish.
∎

\lemSoundnessResult

*

Proof.

By mathematical induction on the length of the evaluation sequence.

Case the length is 0:
:

Obvious since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[]
.

Case the length is more than 0:
:

We are given

•:

\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,

•:

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
, and

•:

S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

for some
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
By case analysis on the evaluation rule applied to
\mathit{f}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}}
.

Case (
E_Blame
):
:

Obvious.

Case (
E_Step
):
:

We have

•:

\mathit{f}=\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}]
,

•:

\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])
, and

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

for some
\mathit{E}
,
\mathit{f^{\prime}}
, and
\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
By case analysis on the reduction rule applied to derive

\mathit{f^{\prime}}\,\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Case (
R_InstBase
):
:

We are given

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}}=w^{\prime}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}
,

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=w^{\prime}
, and

•:

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=\iota]

for some
w^{\prime}
,
\iota
,
\ell
, and
\mathit{X}
.

Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
or
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Case
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
:
:

Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
we have
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X})=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X})=\iota
.
Thus,

\begin{array}[]{lll}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f})&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X})]\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}):\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\iota]\\
&\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime})]\qquad\text{(since $S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime})$ is a value by Lemma~\ref{lem:value_any_subst})}\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\qquad\text{(since $S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=\iota]$ and
$S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X})=\iota$)}\\
\end{array}

Since
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
there exist some
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
such that

•:

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and

•:

S_{{\mathrm{1}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
.
Here,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})&=&\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\\
&=&\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&=&\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&=&\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&=&\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&\supseteq&\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&=&\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\end{array}

Thus, by the IH, there exists some
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that

S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
and

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\subseteq\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and

\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset

for any
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Then,
we have
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))=S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))
and

S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\,S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))
.

Let
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
If
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, then
\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))=\emptyset
.
If
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, then
\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
by the IH.

Case
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
:
:

Since
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
are disjoint,

\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Thus,

\begin{array}[]{lll}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f})&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}:\iota\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}])\\
&\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}]))\qquad\text{($S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime})$ is a value by Lemma~\ref{lem:value_any_subst})}\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}]))\qquad\text{(since $S_{{\mathrm{1}}}$ is generated by DTI)}\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\end{array}

Since
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
there exist some
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that

•:

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and

•:

S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
.
Here,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})&=&\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\\
&=&\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\qquad(\text{by the discussion above})\\
&\supseteq&\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\{\mathit{X}\}\\
&=&\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\qquad(\text{since $\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})$})\end{array}

By the IH, there exists some
S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that

S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
and

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\subseteq\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and,
for any
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
,
\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
.

Let
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
We have
\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
.
Thus, it suffices to show that
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))
.
It is obvious because
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is generated by

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
where
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))
.

Case (
R_InstArrow
):
:

We are given

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}}=w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X}
,

•:

\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}=w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, and

•:

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]

for some
w^{\prime}
,
\mathit{X}
,
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and fresh
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and

\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.
Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
or
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Case
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
:
:

Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
we have
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X})=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.
Since
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
are fresh,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\not\in\,\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f})
.
Since
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\subseteq\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f})
, we have

\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
Let
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
be a type substitution such that

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})\setminus(\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})\setminus\{\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\})
and

S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{Y})=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{Y})
for any
\mathit{Y}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Note that
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
and
S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.
Since
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
there exist some
S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that

•:

S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,

•:

S_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, and

•:

S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Here,

\begin{array}[]{lll}S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f})&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X})]\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\\
&\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}[]\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\\
&&\quad\text{($S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime})$ is a value by Lemma~\ref{lem:value_any_subst})}\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E})[S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(w^{\prime}):\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\\
&=&S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}])\\
&&\quad\text{(since $\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})$ are fresh type variables if any)}\\
&=&[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}])\\
&=&S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}:\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell}\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\star\!\rightarrow\!\star\Rightarrow^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}][\mathit{X}:=\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\!\rightarrow\!\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}])\\
&=&S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\end{array}

We have
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])=\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
.
Here,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})&=&\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]))\\
&=&(\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\})\cup\{\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\}\\
&\supseteq&(\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\})\cup\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\\
&=&((\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[w^{\prime}])\cup\{\mathit{X}\})\setminus\{\mathit{X}\})\cup\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\\
&=&(\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}}])\setminus\{\mathit{X}\})\cup\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\\
&\supseteq&(\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\setminus\{\mathit{X}\})\cup\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\\
&=&\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cup\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\end{array}

Thus, by the IH,

S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
for some
S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that

\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\subseteq\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and,
for any
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,

\textit{ftv}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
.
We have
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{f})\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
.

Let
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
If
\mathit{X^{\prime}}=\mathit{X}
, then
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and so

\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Otherwise, if
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\neq\mathit{X}
, then
\mathit{X^{\prime}}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Since
S^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}})=S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}})
, we have

\textit{ftv}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{X^{\prime}}))\cap\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
by the IH.

Case
\mathit{X}\,\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
:
:

Since
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{2}}})
are disjoint,

\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Furthermore,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})

since
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{E}[\mathit{f^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}])\,\xmapsto{\mathmakebox[0.4em]{}S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\mathmakebox[0.3em]{}}\hskip-3.99994pt{}^{\ast}\hskip 1.99997pt\,\mathit{r}
,
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\mathit{X}:=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})]\uplus S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
Since
S_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus S_{{\mathrm{2}}}=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\circ S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
, we have

S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X})=S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\!\rightarrow\!S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.
Since
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
are fresh,
we can suppose that
\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\not\in\,\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f})
.
Since
\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})\subseteq\textit{ftv}\,(\mathit{f})
, we have

\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\not\in\,\textit{dom}\,(S_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Thus,
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
and
S^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=S_{{\mathrm{2}}}(\mathit{X_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

since
